# Planing Ledger

> Ledger v2 — ClaudeQB. Created by Step 2. Body language: English (default contract).

## 1. Purpose

This ledger is the durable record of planning and implementation continuity for `ishtar`. It exists so that a replanning run or a Step 4 implementation session can determine what was planned, what was applied, what was verified, and what remains blocked, without reconstructing that history from a conversation.

It is supporting evidence, not absolute truth. Where this ledger and the current repository state disagree, the repository wins and the ledger is corrected. Step 4 appends concise implementation summaries after verified slices or stop events; Step 2 and Step 3 update planning state.

The project's own history is the reason this file matters. Autopsy `AUTOPSY-P0-02` records a three-step misattribution caused by state not being captured before and after changes, and Project-Comprehension `QAS-03` records that findings deferred to later in a session are the ones lost to context truncation. A ledger that is written during work rather than after it is the countermeasure.

Not corrected in `docs/release-checklist.md` because that file belongs to this sub-plan and an implementation run does not amend a sub-plan; routed to the next Step 3 audit. **`VAL-MANUAL` COMPLETE 2026-09-07 (`IMPL-85`): all seven user rows exercised, six clean and one partial.** `PC-05` PASS — `8192x6144` = 50.33 MP from the JPEG SOF marker (0xFFC0), black fraction 0.000008, and the same press repaired `FIND-3.9-02`. `PC-03` PASS — EXIF `ExposureTime` 0.04 s = 1/25 s identical in `stock` and `dcgB`; the pair is well controlled (ISO 1406 vs 1409, BrightnessValue -2.05 both, mean luma 103.17 vs 103.64). `PC-02` EXERCISED but its quantitative claim deliberately NOT re-measured: a matched-exposure pair was captured, which is what the row asks, and an indicative flat-tile noise proxy gave dcgB 1.640 vs stock 1.693, about 3 pct lower — directionally consistent with "25 pct less noise" but far too weak to confirm it from a handheld pair of 9 flat tiles at ISO ~1400 with pipeline denoising, so the section 7 waiver and the original one-time measurement remain the authority. `PC-09` PASS — four tiles, exactly one active, matching the current mode, which is the half the "partial, IMPL-75" detector cannot see. `PC-11` PASS — Action advanced `50mp` -> `dcgB` and reported it. `PC-12` PASS — module set to `tr` with the system left at `en-US`; `action.sh info` field 4 followed the module and `setmode.sh` printed Turkish, which is the independence `C-12` claims. `PC-13` PARTIAL, raised as **`FIND-9.2-07`** — dimensions correct (`3840x2160` and `1920x1080` from the MP4 `tkhd` box) but the frame rate is 30, not 60, confirmed by two independent methods (sample-count/duration 30.05 fps; `stts` delta 2997 at timescale 90000 = 30.030 fps) after the user explicitly selected 60 fps. **The module is exonerated by a discriminator:** the same recording in `stock`, where both HAL and sensor are unpatched, also gave 30.03 fps, so the behaviour is mode-independent. Incidentally settled by the switches this required: **`PC-08` confirmed ON DEVICE** — `50mp` is stock sensor `9e4d0fe1...` with fullres HAL `fe0245a4...`, `dcgB` is dcgB sensor `ee73da4e...` with stock HAL `c3fa63a8...`, so the patched layers swap and are never both patched (Ontology invariant 4 by observation); and **`PC-01`'s scope note is RESOLVED** — in `dcgB` the live sensor blob equals the selected mode, so `C-01` is exercised on its own terms, which the `50mp` snapshot cannot do by design. **Tree scope decision (F6.1-03), stated definitively.** Only the two device trees need the entry. `refs/a16_sm8550/proprietary-files.txt` and `common_sm8550/proprietary-files.txt` do not: both contain zero occurrences of `aon_front`, because the blob is device-specific ODM-partition content and those two are platform trees. Checked, not assumed.

**Unverified status (F6.1-07), stated so nobody reads this as a proven fix.** The entry changes what a build would produce. No build has run, and none can on this host — WSL2 is absent, which is what Phase 7 exists to change. Whether the built image actually creates `/vendor/lib64/camera/aon_front.pb` is unknown until then, and Faz 6.2 registers that verification against Phase 7. `EV-11` is therefore marked `confirmed (as an edit) / unverified (as a fix)`.

**Two version-control notes on this slice, both consequences of IMPL-01.** First, the `device_ishtar/` edit is left **uncommitted** in that repository. It is tracked there and fully recoverable (`git -C device_ishtar diff` shows it, `git checkout --` reverts it), but committing onto a branch that tracks an AOSPA remote is a divergence for the user to decide, not a side effect of running the queue. Second, `refs/a16_ishtar/proprietary-files.txt` is **unversioned**: IMPL-01 excluded all of `refs/` as externally sourced, so that mirrored edit has no history anywhere. It is a single line whose exact source is cited above, so it is cheap to reproduce — but this is a real interaction between Faz 1.1 and Faz 6.1 that neither sub-plan anticipated, and it is recorded here rather than left to be discovered.

| IMPL-04 | 2026-08-30 | `Planner-docs/Faz-2-Plans/Faz2.1-device-smoke-script.md` | F2.1-01 through F2.1-07: fixed the output contract, built `scripts/smoke.sh`, extracted the camera metadata, added the application-layer fields, reviewed every command for read-only status, validated with a real single-variable mode change, and established the snapshot convention. | Device `dc27e5d0` (`ishtar`, 2304FPN6DC). **`VAL-SMOKE`**: one invocation returns all nine field groups, 150 comparable lines including 110 stream-configuration rows. **`VAL-IDEMPOTENT`**: two consecutive runs produced a byte-identical comparable region. **`VAL-SENSITIVITY`**: switching `dcgB`→`dcgA` produced a diff of exactly three lines — `mode.file`, `sensor.live.md5`, `sensor.live.variant` — and nothing else moved. **`VAL-READONLY`**: see the per-command verdict below. Device was restored to `dcgB` and the restored snapshot is byte-identical to the baseline. Snapshots in `snapshots/`. | Complete. All nine acceptance criteria pass. |

**Read-only review (F2.1-05), per-command verdict.** The script issues exactly four device-side pipelines. Every command in them is one of `cat`, `ls`, `md5sum`, `stat`, `basename`, `grep`, `sed`, `awk`, `cut`, `sort`, `tr`, `wc`, `echo`, `printf`, `head`, `dumpsys`, `pm path`, `pm list` — all read-only. Verified by inspection that `umount`, `stop`, `start`, `am force-stop`, `setprop`, `rm`, `mv`, `cp`, `dd`, `chmod`, and `chown` appear **only inside comments**, and that the sole occurrences of `mount` are reads of `/proc/1/mountinfo` and the `*.mount.count` output labels. The script pushes nothing to the device and writes nothing outside the host-side `snapshots/` directory. The mode change used to prove sensitivity was made by the operator through the module's own `setmode.sh`, never by the script.

**Snapshot convention (F2.1-07).** `snapshots/<UTC yyyymmdd-HHMM>__<subplan id>__<label>.txt`, for example `20260830-1840__faz2.1__baseline-dcgB.txt`. Chronologically sortable, greppable by sub-plan, and human-labelled, so a Faz 3.3 snapshot can be diffed against a Faz 2.2 baseline weeks later. Each file carries a non-comparable header (timestamp, serial) above a `--- BEGIN COMPARABLE ---` marker; diffs are taken on the marked region only.

**Four things the first real run established, three of which correct the record.**
1. **`H-10` is contradicted and `OQ-10` is answered no.** The device is on `dcgB` (live blob md5 matches `dcgB.bin`); the workspace `mode` file says `stock`. The workspace file tracks the last host-side edit, not device state, and must never be read as a mirror of it. `EV-19` moves from `tentative` to `contradicted`.
2. **`EV-14` is confirmed and `EV-03` is corrected.** The device runs module `v2.0`, `versionCode 11`. Released v2.1 has indeed never been flashed, and the workspace `module.prop` that reads v2.1/12 describes the workspace, not the phone.
3. **The patched AGC build is installed, not reverted.** The installed `org.codeaurora.snapcam` APK md5 is `ce65f862c990e2acc02be356f75a7122`, which matches `AGC8.4.300_V9.6_50MP_patched.apk` byte for byte — not the stock V9.6 build. Section 6 of this ledger previously recorded the patched AGC as "currently reverted on the device"; that is corrected below. The HAL *is* reverted (`hal.state = stock`), so only half of that claim was right — precisely the kind of split state `AUTOPSY-P0-02` describes.
4. **`CQ-03` is answered.** "What proves a mode switch worked" now has an answer: `scripts/smoke.sh`, demonstrated rather than asserted.

**New open question `OQ-17`, raised by this run.** The project's "camera 3" shorthand does not survive measurement. `dumpsys media.camera` maps framework device 3 to HAL id 9, which is Front-facing at 3264x2448 and cannot be the IMX989; the rear 4096x3072 array sits on HAL id 0 and ids 2-7 and 10. Rather than guess, the script reports all thirteen HAL devices and prints full rows for the lowest Back-facing id under a stated rule. Settle `OQ-17` by taking a smoke snapshot with the HAL in `fullres` and diffing: the device whose `streamcfg` count moves is the one the patch modifies. Faz 4.1 needs this before it can state its target array count as a number.

**`AUDIT-FIX-02` decision, recorded here as the audit requires before Faz 3.3.** The audit asked that either the `Faz9.2` recovery runbook be pulled forward, or the deferral be recorded explicitly with what stands in its place. **Decision: deferral recorded, with three concrete mitigations now in hand, and it is the user's call whether that is sufficient before Faz 3.3.** What stands in place of the runbook: (i) `scripts/smoke.sh` now exists and gives a before/after bracket that did not exist when the audit was written, and it reports `hal.mount.count`, which is exactly the stacked-mount false-restore case `Faz9.2` was worried about; (ii) the module's own `hal_set stock` path plus `hal_umount_all`, which loops rather than issuing a single `umount`, precisely because a single one leaves the file patched; (iii) the HAL is currently `stock` with the module at v2.0 and working, so a known-good state is recorded and reproducible. What is still missing is a runbook verified **independent of module-provided commands** — if the module itself is the thing that is broken, every recovery path above runs through it. That gap is real and is not closed by this slice.


| IMPL-05 | 2026-08-30 | `Planner-docs/Faz-1-Plans/Faz1.5-static-safety-probes.md` | F1.5-01 through F1.5-07: read `customize.sh`, `post-fs-data.sh`, `halpatch.sh`, `action.sh`, `service.sh`, and `setmode.sh` in full and `webroot/index.html` by exhaustive call-site trace; listed both recent archives by entry name; answered four ontology questions, two comprehension questions, and three hypotheses; produced the three-script discipline table; routed three findings. | **`VAL-STATIC`**: every answer cites a file and line range, recorded in the rows themselves. **`VAL-SEC`**: the WebUI trace is exhaustive — 4 `exec()` sites, 2 `run()` callers, all arguments resolved to source literals; the three-script fail-closed review is below. **`VAL-DOCS`**: `Project-Ontology.md` OQ-05..OQ-08 and `Project-Comprehension.md` CQ-11, CQ-12, H-06..H-08 rewritten, both files unchanged in line count, 4 and 5 lines changed respectively. No device, build, or user action was involved. | Complete. All eight acceptance criteria pass. |

**Fail-closed discipline review (F1.5-04), three scripts against the `setmode.sh` reference.** The reference pattern is precondition, whitelist, size check, lock, write, **verify**, report — `setmode.sh:20-21, 178-193, 196-197, 199-202, 252, 258-263`.

| Script | Verdict | Guards present | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `post-fs-data.sh` | Fail-closed, one gap | Target exists `:30`; md5 with a size fallback `:38-51`; blob exists `:54-57`; blob size `:58-61`; `mkdir` and `cat` both checked `:65-66`; HAL target md5 `:102`; seed and set both checked `:110-125`. Every failure path exits **0** deliberately, so the module is not marked broken and stock behaviour survives. | **FIND-1.5-03.** `cat "$SRC" > "$ACT"` at `:66` is bind-mounted at `:72` with **no post-copy md5 or size verification**, unlike `setmode.sh:258-263`. `$SRC` is size-checked, the copy is not. A short write (no space, I/O error that still exits 0) would be mounted as the live sensor driver. |
| `halpatch.sh` | Fail-closed, strong | `hal_set:101` verifies md5 after every write; `hal_seed:115` verifies after copy; `hal_seed:107-109` refuses to seed while mounted or from a non-stock source; `hal_umount_all:43-52` loops rather than issuing a single `umount`, because one leaves the file patched (measured). | None material. `hal_write:76` returns 0 unconditionally and swallows `dd` stderr, but it has exactly **one** caller, `hal_set:100`, which verifies on the next line. Safe by caller, not by contract. |
| `customize.sh` | Fail-closed on the sensor, deliberately fail-open on the HAL | Aborts on wrong device `:7-10`, missing driver `:16-18`, and driver md5 mismatch `:23-28`. The HAL branch `:40-50` warns without aborting, and the rationale is stated at `:30-32`: DCG/LN live in the sensor layer and are unaffected, only 50MP is lost. That is a considered decision, not an oversight. | Does not normalize install state — moot, see OQ-07. The live defect is the **claim** at `:62`, FIND-1.5-01. |

**Three findings, each routed rather than fixed (F1.5-07).** This sub-phase's out-of-scope section forbids remediation here.

- **FIND-1.5-01 — `customize.sh:62` prints a guarantee nothing implements.** The installer tells the user "Baslangic modu: ilk kurulumda stock; guncellemede mevcut mod". The first half is true only by accident (the archive ships no `mode`, so `post-fs-data.sh:23-27` defaults to `stock`). The second half has **no implementing code**: no path preserves an existing `mode` across an update, and both Magisk and KernelSU stage a module update into a fresh directory, so the previous `mode` is not carried into the new one. **Consequence for the next queue item:** the device is currently on `dcgB` with module v2.0; flashing v2.1 in Faz 2.2 should be expected to come back as `stock`, not `dcgB`. Routed to **Faz 2.2**, which must record the pre-flash mode and either accept the reset or preserve the file by hand; the wording fix is routed to **Faz 9.3** (stale-claim consolidation).
- **FIND-1.5-02 — `webroot/index.html:276` renders device-derived text as HTML.** `meta.innerHTML` interpolates `mount` and `md5`, both split out of `action.sh info` at `:272`. **Not exploitable today**: `action.sh:186` constrains those fields to one of two literals (`aktif`/`yok`) and to `md5sum | cut` output. It is nonetheless the only site whose safety rests on the producer's output shape rather than on a check in the consumer, and it is the one place where a future `info` field could turn into script execution inside a root-privileged WebUI. The other two `innerHTML` sites, `:237` and `:314`, interpolate only the `I18N` literal table and are clean. Routed to **Faz 9.2** as a hardening item (use `textContent`, or validate the fields on read).
- **FIND-1.5-03 — unverified copy before bind mount, `post-fs-data.sh:66`.** Detailed in the table above. Routed to **Faz 2.3**, the ten-mode regression pass, which is the first slice that exercises this path repeatedly and is the natural place to add the verification and prove it.

**Four questions closed and one hypothesis premise corrected.** `OQ-05` through `OQ-08` and `CQ-11`, `CQ-12` are answered with cited evidence. `H-07` and `H-08` are **confirmed**. `H-06` is **contradicted in its premise but its conclusion holds**: `customize.sh` does not normalize `mode`, yet shipping developer state is harmless anyway, because measurement of the archives shows the state is never shipped. Recording that distinction matters — the hypothesis would read as plainly "confirmed" if only its conclusion were checked, and the reason it holds is not the reason it asserted.

**`AUTOPSY-P3-03` is contradicted for the shipped artifact.** The sub-plan expected this probe to confirm a packaging defect. Measurement says otherwise: `unzip -Z1` on `ishtar-cam-modes-v2.1.zip` and `v2.0.zip` shows neither ships `mode`, `lang`, `active/`, `active_hal/`, `tile-app/debug.keystore`, `tile-app/out/`, or `tools/__pycache__/`, all of which exist in the source directory. The packaging step already excludes runtime state and the keystore — the latter independently re-confirming IMPL-02's CLEAN verdict by a second method.


| IMPL-06 | 2026-08-30 | `Planner-docs/Faz-1-Plans/Faz1.3-patch-table-format-contract.md` | F1.3-01 through F1.3-08: transcribed the grammar from `hal_write`, documented the four-byte index convention and its alignment constraint, recorded the escape encoding with its measured toybox failure, stated the verification and chaining model, resolved the duplicated-constant question, answered `OQ-05` in place, wrote the extension and location rules, and assessed sensor-blob applicability. | **`VAL-ROUNDTRIP`**: four records decoded by hand to instructions and back. `hal-fullres.patch:12,14,15` all decode as `LDR Wd,[Xn,#0x3d84]` -> `MOV Wd,#0`; `imm12` = `0xF61` = 3937, scaled x4 = `0x3D84`, matching that table's own header claim at its line 2. Line 15 differs only in base register (X12 vs X24), which is why its patched column repeats line 12's — the rewrite depends only on `Rd`. `hal-raw50-deneme.patch:25` decodes as `CMP W11,#2` -> `CMP W11,#5`, matching its stated intent at lines 3-5. **`VAL-DOCS`**: 10 sections, every rule cites a parser line; markdown table column counts verified against their headers. | Complete. All eight acceptance criteria pass. |

**Chaining, confirmed by measurement rather than assumption (F1.3-04).** `hal-raw50-deneme.patch:23` names its base as `fe0245a4720fb07b01d96deef47f5569`, which is exactly the result md5 `hal-fullres.patch:10` produces. The experimental table is therefore meaningless applied to a stock binary — its "stock" column holds the bytes as they exist *after* the fullres patch. Nothing in `hal_write` enforces this: the parser skips `#` lines and so never reads a base md5 at all. The order is enforced only by `hal_set:101` rejecting a wrong result md5.

**Duplicated-constant decision (F1.3-05), stated so Faz 4.3 does not have to re-litigate it.** The stock/fullres md5 pair exists in three places. `halpatch.sh:32-33` is **authoritative** — it is the only copy with runtime effect. The table header comments are **not redundant** and must not be deleted: they record provenance, answer a different question, and are the copy that travels with a table when it is relocated. `customize.sh:37-38` is a display mirror. All three must change in one commit; no automated check enforces that today, and the assertion belongs in Faz 9.2.

**Two format hazards named, neither fixable here (out of scope: this sub-phase documents, Faz 4.3 changes content).** First, `hal_write:71` `[ -n "$ep" ] || continue` means a **two-column record is silently dropped** — no error, and the only symptom is a result md5 that does not match. Second, a **fourth column** lands inside `$ep` because `read` assigns the remainder to the last variable; it survives today only because `dd bs=4 count=1` truncates the write to four bytes. Both are recorded as breaking-change rules in section 6 of the contract.

**Sensor-blob gap recorded (F1.3-08), not acted on.** Sensor modes are whole-file replacements guarded **only** by `EXPECT_SIZE=1062250`. There is no expected-md5 constant for any of the nine blobs. `setmode.sh:258-263` verifies after writing, but against an md5 computed from the source itself — which proves the copy succeeded, not that the source was correct. A corrupted blob of the right size passes every check. Converting the blobs to patch tables is assessed and **not recommended** (they are large, non-word-aligned register tables); the cheap fix is nine recorded md5 constants, routed to Faz 9.1/9.2.

Each verified slice or stop event appends a row here recording the sub-plan path, the slice performed, the validation evidence produced, and the resulting status change in section 4.

**Confirmed and contradicted claims from IMPL-01.** `EV-01` is inverted and stays `confirmed`: the workspace is now a repository. `CQ-02` moves from `answered (negatively)` to `answered (positively)`. `EV-18` is contradicted as stated and replaced by measurement: the workspace holds approximately 7.3 GiB, not roughly 465 MB — the earlier figure summed root-level files only and counted no directory, and 454.4 MiB of root-level files is what it actually measured. `AUTOPSY-P0-01`, the project's highest-severity finding, is resolved.

**Two scope deviations, recorded rather than applied silently.** `device_ishtar/` was excluded although Faz 1.1 section 4 lists it as tracked content: it is its own clean git clone of AOSPA upstream on branch `vauxite`, with full history and a remote, so it already has the rollback path this sub-phase creates, and nesting it would have stored a gitlink that restores no file content. **Faz 6.1 edits that tree and must commit there, not in the parent repository.** Conversely, `12mpx_agc_config.agc` and `50mpx_agc_config.agc` were tracked although the sub-plan lists them in neither its tracked set nor its exclusion set: 220 KB of hand-tuned UTF-8 XML that no script in the workspace regenerates. Both deviations are argued in `UNTRACKED-BY-DESIGN.md` section 7.

**One hazard found and fixed inside the slice.** This host has `core.autocrlf=true`, which would have checked out every tracked script with CRLF line endings. `ishtar-cam-modes/post-fs-data.sh` and its siblings are executed by the on-device Magisk shell, which does not tolerate that, so a checkout would have restored a broken module — a rollback path that does not roll back. `.gitattributes` now defaults to no conversion, so a checkout returns the committed bytes, and pins `eol=lf` for `.sh`, `.prop`, `.patch`, `mode`, and `lang`. Verified: `git ls-files --eol` reports `i/lf w/lf` for every one of them.

**One local configuration change.** The host had no `user.name` or `user.email`. Both were set **repo-local only** (`git config --local`), not globally, as `ahmet <ahmetozgurc40@gmail.com>`. Change it with `git config --local user.name ...` if that attribution is wrong.

Two recording rules apply and both come from the project's own history rather than from convention. Every located binary address is written into `Planner-docs/Project-Ontology.md` during the session it is found, per Main-Planing risk `R10` and Project-Comprehension `QAS-03`. Every probe that resolves updates the confidence column in `Planner-docs/Project-Comprehension.md` and the corresponding question status in `Planner-docs/Project-Ontology.md` in the same session, per the recording rule stated in that document's section 8.

**Faz 2.3 outcome in one line: the module is correct; the two failures found are at its edges and neither is caused by it.**

**The pass began by catching its own recording gap.** The user ran the first tile block before each switch was bracketed, so rows 3-5 had manual verdicts and no snapshots. Rather than infer them from the end state, they were marked `missing` and re-measured, and the affected rows carry **split provenance** in the record — manual half from the tile run, automated half from a later CLI run. This is the `AUTOPSY-P0-02` pattern the sub-plan's section 11 predicts, caught during the run rather than after it.

**The `ln2` / `ln2f` tile correction earned its place immediately.** The user tapped the QuickSettings button labelled **LN2**, which `Ln2Tile.java`'s `mode()` sets to **`ln2f`**, not `ln2`. Without the correction carried in the handoff, that tap would have been recorded against the `ln2` row — a wrong mode against a wrong row, with `ln2` never tested at all. `ln2` was subsequently driven by CLI, which is the only way to reach it, and it turned out to be one of the two failing modes.

**`FIND-2.3-02` — `ln2` and `ln4` crash AGC, and the correlation is exact.** Both throw `java.lang.NullPointerException: Attempt to invoke virtual method 'boolean com.google.googlex.gcam.Gcam.f()' on a null object reference` and land on `com.agc.DefaultErrorActivity`. They are also the only two modes advertising a **split** RAW format — `37` at `4096x2304`, `38` at `4096x3072` — which corroborates `Kullanim.txt:36-38`'s statement that they patch **sensor mode 1 only**. Across the full population: 8 unified-metadata modes open AGC, 8/8; 2 split-metadata modes crash it, 2/2. Causation is **not** proven — the split was never manipulated independently of the blob — but the mechanism is plausible against the libgcam static-metadata invariant, and `ln2` gives Phase 3 a **reproducible one-command negative control** for the very invariant that has been blocking it. Priority stays low: both modes are marked `ESKI/REFERANS` and `Kullanmayin`, and `overrideForceSensorMode` was confirmed empty. OpenCamera opens and captures normally in `ln2`, so the camera stack is healthy.

**`FIND-2.3-03` — AGC's video section is black, and the module is not the cause.** Measured in four states: `dcgB` after reboot 1, `stock` after the documented workaround, `50mp` after reboot 2, and `stock` after reboot 2. All four black. The signature is constant: `camxhal3.cpp:2834 configure_streams() HalOp: End CONFIG failed, logicalCameraId: 0, cameraId: 2`, with `FPS: 14 ~ 30`. It reproduces in `stock` — the module's own no-op configuration, sensor and HAL both unmodified — so nothing the module does distinguishes failing from passing states. The user confirms AGC video worked previously, making it a real regression, but not one attributable here. The preference trap was investigated and **excluded**: both keys read `0`. Cause not established; the cheapest first control is installing stock AGC `fa4066eb…`, which is a Phase 3 action. User impact is low — the user does not use AGC video and intends to use `mcpro24fps`, which is **not currently installed**.

**Two documentation claims failed under test.** `Kullanim.txt:45` promises `setmode.sh stock` restores video **with no reboot**; applied exactly against a verified clean `stock` state, it did not (`FIND-2.3-06`). And `Kullanim.txt:209` gives `CSIPHYHw:4 AvailableResource=0` as the signature of the hardware-allocation leak; that PHY reads `0` **on a clean boot** with an unrelated failure present, so the stated signature is not diagnostic and would produce a misdiagnosis (`FIND-2.3-04`). A third claim is refined rather than refuted: the pink banding at `Kullanim.txt:38` is conditional on the consumer selecting sensor mode 1, and OpenCamera showed no pink tint in `ln2` (`FIND-2.3-05`). All three to Faz 9.3.

**`FIND-2.3-01` — the RAW-depth discriminator is not reliable, which matters because this pass was told to use it.** The handoff instructed that a DCG blob advertises `38` and a non-DCG blob `37`, at no reboot cost. The identical `ln2f` blob — same md5, mount count 1 in both snapshots — advertised `37` at 16:51 and `38` at 16:55. Two controlled re-tests eliminated the obvious explanations: a tile-driven change with the camera app never opened read correctly, and so did one with a live session held open. The anomaly did not reproduce and its cause is unknown. `FIND-2.2-02` is **qualified, not overturned** — blob content does drive the advertisement, but something else can leave it stale, so any later phase reading advertised metadata after a mode change must confirm it republished. No mode's verdict depends on it; every SMOKE verdict rests on values read off the live mount.

**`FIND-1.5-03` is measured and stays open as a code change.** Both reboots mounted a byte-exact blob — `ee73da4e…` at 1062250/1062250 for `dcgB`. Reading both paths sharpened the finding into an asymmetry: `setmode.sh:262-270` re-reads the mounted target and compares md5, aborting on mismatch, while `post-fs-data.sh:66` copies and bind-mounts at line 72 with no such check, its pre-copy guards checking the *source* rather than the *result*. A latent robustness gap with no observed symptom. Routed to **Faz 9.1**; the fix is to mirror the interactive path's comparison into the boot path.

**On the automated preview check, and why it did not replace the user.** AGC was driven with `monkey`, the crash buffer and resumed activity were read, and a raw `screencap` frame was sampled for luminance across the central 50% of the display. That instrumentation caught both `ln2`/`ln4` crashes. It also returned **`LIVE` for the black video preview** — `mean=8.1`, `nonblack_frac=0.099`, but AGC's bright chrome around the dead surface pushed the variance past the threshold. The one black preview in the entire pass was found by the user's eyes and missed by the instrument. Recorded rather than retuned, because it bounds what the automated column is worth and justifies `VAL-MANUAL` remaining user-performed.

**Boundary note, third session running.** `Planner-docs/Project-Ontology.md` still carries the unedited `OQ-17` row from IMPL-12, and now also needs invariant 3, 4 and 5 updated from this pass — invariants 3 and 4 hold under direct test, and invariant 5 is **unassessable** on this build rather than confirmed or refuted, since the 60 fps profiles were never reachable. That file is outside Faz 2.3's section 9 boundary, so it is again left unedited. `AUDIT-FIX-04` in `Sub-Planing-Audit.md` is in the same position. Both should be attached to the next sub-plan whose boundary includes them; `Faz 4.1` already needs the ontology file for its guard sizing.

**`FIND-1.5-01` is upgraded from a static reading to a certainty, and its mechanism is not the one the sub-plan assumed.** Faz 1.5 read `customize.sh:62` — `ui_print "- Baslangic modu: ilk kurulumda stock; guncellemede mevcut mod"` — and found no code implementing the second clause. This run confirms that from the release artifact itself: a grep of the archive's `customize.sh` for any statement writing `mode` returns nothing, and the archive contains no `mode` entry. The sub-plan's F2.2-01 expected to *read a packaged value*; there is none to read. The post-install mode is therefore decided by `post-fs-data.sh:14`, which does `MODE=$(cat "$MODDIR/mode" 2>/dev/null)` against a fresh staging directory, gets the empty string, and falls to the `*)` branch at line 23, which sets `MODE=stock`, `SENSOR_MODE=stock`, `HAL_WANT=stock`. The device will come up `stock`, by the fail-safe default rather than by a shipped value. **`dcgB` -> `stock` is a predicted and explained diff line, not a regression.**

**`AUDIT-FIX-02`'s precondition for Faz 3.3 is settled here, ahead of schedule, and it holds in both directions.** The Step 3 finding's carried-forward condition was that the "disable, do not repair" recovery argument survives only if Faz 3.3 patches the module's `active_hal/` copy rather than writing `/odm` directly. `/proc/1/mountinfo` on the live device shows the HAL bind mount source is `/data/adb/modules/ishtar_cam_modes/active_hal/camera.qcom.so`, mounted onto `/odm/lib64/hw/camera.qcom.so`, and the sensor mount source is `.../active/com.qti.sensormodule.ishtar_semco_imx989_wide.bin` onto its `/odm` path. A bind mount is the same inode, so a write addressed to the `/odm` path while the module is active lands in `active_hal/` regardless of which path the patcher names — the distinction the finding worried about does not exist while the mount is up. With the module disabled there is no mount, and `/odm` is a read-only vendor mount, so a stray write fails rather than damages. The recovery primitive is therefore sound on both branches. It is still unproven by execution; proving it is proposed as part of this sub-phase.

**One collateral fact worth the address book.** `active_hal/` exists on the device and is populated, confirming the README's claim that it is seeded on-device from the device's own stock HAL and never exists in the workspace. Both bind mounts originate from the same device `254:41`, i.e. `/data`.


**`AUDIT-FIX-02`'s recovery primitive is proven by execution rather than argued.** It was proven *before* the flash and from `dcgB` rather than from `stock`, which is the stronger of the two available forms: from `stock` the module mounts `blobs/stock.bin` over a byte-identical vendor blob, so no checksum moves and only the mount counts discriminate. Three snapshots, two reboots.

- `snapshots/20260831-1533__faz2.2__pre-flash-v2.0-dcgB.txt` — module active, `dcgB`.
- `snapshots/20260831-1539__faz2.2__recovery-disabled.txt` — after `touch $MODDIR/disable` and a reboot. `sensor.live.md5` moves `ee73da4e8f4f542161e7faa2a8a2141e` -> `9e4d0fe1a8bf2eb4146c9917a0ac2c4c`, `sensor.live.variant` `dcgB` -> `stock`, and **both** `sensor.mount.count` and `hal.mount.count` go `1` -> `0`. The genuine vendor blob is what the camera sees. `$MODDIR/mode` still reads `dcgB` — disabling suppresses the mount without destroying module state.
- `snapshots/20260831-1541__faz2.2__recovery-restored.txt` — after `rm $MODDIR/disable` and a reboot. The comparable region is **byte-identical** to the pre-flash snapshot.

The primitive is therefore both effective and exactly reversible: `adb shell su -c 'touch /data/adb/modules/ishtar_cam_modes/disable && reboot'` returns the device to genuine vendor state, and removing the flag returns it to the precise prior state. Recovery is "do not mount", and it is provided by the root manager without executing any module code. **`Faz 9.2` does not need to be pulled forward.**

**The `Faz 3.3` precondition attached to that decision is confirmed, and it holds on both branches.** The condition was that the argument survives only if `Faz3.3` patches the module's `active_hal/` copy rather than writing `/odm` directly. `Planner-docs/Faz-3-Plans/Faz3.3-single-word-patch-and-metadata-proof.md:56` states that `hal_write` writes to `$MODDIR/active_hal/camera.qcom.so` "rather than to the live target". Independently, `/proc/1/mountinfo` shows the live `/odm` HAL *is* a bind mount of that same file, so the two addresses are one inode while the module is active, and with it disabled `/odm` is a read-only vendor mount where a stray write fails rather than damages. One caution carried forward from that same sub-plan at line 29: an experimental entry changes the copy's checksum, `hal_state` then classifies it `unknown`, and `hal_set` **re-seeds** it — so `Faz3.3` must account for the module undoing its own experiment.

**`FIND-2.2-01` (new, P2, open) — the v2.1 installer aborts any upgrade performed from a non-stock sensor mode.** `customize.sh:20` reads `md5sum /odm/lib64/camera/com.qti.sensormodule.ishtar_semco_imx989_wide.bin` and `customize.sh:23-27` aborts with "Muhtemelen OTA aldin. Blob'lar yeniden uretilmeli." unless it equals `9e4d0fe1a8bf2eb4146c9917a0ac2c4c`. While the module is active in any mode other than `stock`, that path *is* the module's own bind-mounted blob, so the guard reads the module's work and concludes the vendor partition changed. The asymmetry is the evidence that this is unintended rather than by design: `customize.sh:34-35` reasons about exactly this case for the HAL — "Modul zaten kuruluysa hedef yolda BIZIM yamali kopyamiz bagli olabilir; o durumda okunan md5 fullres'tir. Ikisi de gecerli sayilir" — and `customize.sh:40-44` accordingly accepts both the stock and the fullres checksum. The same allowance was never carried to the sensor guard. **Status: predicted from the artifact's own source, not yet measured.** The abort is fail-closed and harmless, so measuring it costs one refused install. Workaround needs no reboot: `setmode.sh` rewrites the mount's inode in place (`setmode.sh:251`, "Ayni inode'un icerigini degistiriyoruz; mount'a dokunulmuyor"), so `setmode.sh stock` makes the guard pass immediately. Routed to Phase 9 per this sub-plan's section 5, which sends release defects to Phase 9 rather than patching them here.

**`FIND-2.2-02` (new, evidence) — the sensor mode is observable in the camera metadata, not only in the blob checksum.** The disable/enable pair moved two stream-configuration rows that nothing predicted: `camera.hal0`'s `4096 2304` and `4096 3072` entries read format `38` with the `dcgB` blob mounted and format `37` without it. Format codes are not asserted from memory — `agc-50mp-yama/hal-raw50-deneme.patch:5` records `0x25 RAW10, 0x26 RAW12`, i.e. `37` = RAW10 and `38` = RAW12, and `50MP-Entegrasyon-Plani.md:1210-1211` uses the same mapping. So the `dcgB` blob causes the main camera's two full-array entries to be advertised as **RAW12 instead of RAW10**, with the entry count unchanged at 440. This does not contradict IMPL-04's three-line `dcgB`->`dcgA` sensitivity result: both are DCG modes and both would advertise RAW12. It is the first observation in this project of a sensor-layer change that moves the *camera metadata* rather than only the blob identity, it gives `Faz2.3` a second discriminator per mode, and it bears on the AGC work, where `50MP-Entegrasyon-Plani.md:1063` records `pref_fix_resolution_key_N=37` selecting by exactly this format code. A controlled second route — `setmode.sh stock` with the mount left up — is proposed to separate blob content from mount presence.

**`FIND-2.2-03` (new, P3, open) — a documented exclusion that was never implemented.** `README.md`'s exclusions table lists `snapshots/` as absent from version control by design, recovered by re-running `scripts/smoke.sh`. No rule in `.gitignore` implements that: `git status --short` reports all six snapshots as untracked (`??`) rather than ignored, and the three produced by IMPL-04 on 2026-08-30 have sat in that state since. The stated intent and the mechanism disagree, which is the same class of defect as `FIND-1.5-01` — a promise in prose with no code behind it. Nothing is at risk today; the consequence is that a `git clean` would remove them silently and that "clean working tree" claims about this repository require a mental exception. Routed to `Faz 9.3` with the other documentation-versus-reality items, or to a one-line `.gitignore` addition if the next session prefers to close it in place.

**`FIND-2.2-02`'s control experiment is answered: the RAW depth change is caused by blob content, not by mount presence.** IMPL-10 found the device already in the exact configuration the control required — `setmode.sh stock` applied, and `sensor.mount.count` still `1`. Against `snapshots/20260831-1541__faz2.2__recovery-restored.txt` the comparable region moves on exactly five lines: `mode.file` `dcgB` -> `stock`, `sensor.live.md5` `ee73da4e8f4f542161e7faa2a8a2141e` -> `9e4d0fe1a8bf2eb4146c9917a0ac2c4c`, `sensor.live.variant` `dcgB` -> `stock`, and `camera.hal0`'s `4096 2304` and `4096 3072` rows `38` -> `37`. **Both mount counts stay `1` throughout.** In the disable/enable pair that first produced the observation the mounts went `1` -> `0` -> `1`, so mount presence and blob content moved together and could not be separated; here the mount never moves and the format rows still track the blob. The RAW12/RAW10 advertisement is therefore a property of the sensor driver blob's contents. The refresh is not a live re-read: `setmode.sh:262` calls `restart_camera` immediately after rewriting the inode, so the provider republishes the metadata on restart. `Faz 2.3` gains a per-mode discriminator that costs no reboot, and the AGC selector at `50MP-Entegrasyon-Plani.md:1063` (`pref_fix_resolution_key_N=37`) is confirmed to be selecting on a value the sensor mode controls.

**`FIND-2.2-03`'s stated recovery route does not work, which changes its correct resolution.** `README.md` excludes `snapshots/` "by design" and gives "Re-run `scripts/smoke.sh`" as the recovery. That is true only for a snapshot of the *current* device state. Every snapshot this sub-plan depends on is historical — a pre-flash bracket cannot be regenerated once the flash has happened, and `F2.2-07` requires the baseline to be "referenceable by identifier". Implementing the documented `.gitignore` rule would delete the evidence base of Phases 2 through 5. The finding stands, but its resolution is to **correct the README's exclusions table**, not to implement the rule; snapshots are committed deliberately from IMPL-10 onward. Re-routed to `Faz 9.3` with that direction.

**`F2.2-05` — the post-flash diff, with every line accounted for.** Three lines moved between `snapshots/20260831-1557__faz2.2__pre-flash-v2.0-stock-mounted.txt` and `snapshots/20260831-1607__faz2.2__post-flash-v2.1-baseline.txt`, and the comparable region is 150 lines on both sides, so nothing was added or removed.

| Line | Before | After | Account |
|---|---|---|---|
| `module.version` | `v2.0` | `v2.1` | The flash. Predicted. |
| `module.versionCode` | `11` | `12` | The flash. Predicted. |
| `mode.file` | `stock` | *(empty)* | Predicted in direction, **wrong in form.** The value is not the string `stock`; `$MODDIR/mode` **does not exist** — confirmed directly by `ls` on the device, which returns `No such file or directory`. See below. |

Everything the prediction expected to move and did not is itself evidence: `sensor.live.md5`, `sensor.live.variant`, both mount counts, `hal.state`, all thirteen `camera.halN` rows, the 440-row `camera.hal0` stream-configuration block including the two `4096` rows at format `37`, and the whole AGC block. The device was already at `stock` before the flash, so the `dcgB` -> `stock` reset the sub-plan predicted had been spent in advance by IMPL-10 and could not appear here. **`hal.mount.count` is `1` and `hal.state` is `stock` after a wholesale module-directory replacement**, which confirms the README's claim that `active_hal/` is re-seeded on the device from the device's own stock HAL — the directory carries an early-boot timestamp while every flashed file carries the install time.

**The `mode.file` line makes `FIND-1.5-01` measured rather than statically read, and shows its user-facing cost.** The installer prints `- Baslangic modu: ilk kurulumda stock; guncellemede mevcut mod` — "on first install stock; on update, the current mode." `customize.sh` contains **no reference to the `mode` file at all**; a grep of the whole file returns only `set_perm` and `ui_print` lines. KernelSU replaces the module directory wholesale on update, the archive ships no `mode` entry, and nothing copies the old one forward, so the persisted mode is destroyed by every update. `post-fs-data.sh:14` then reads the missing file, gets an empty string, falls to the `*)` branch at line 23, and sets `stock` **in its own variables only** — it never writes the file back, which is why the file stays absent rather than being recreated with `stock`. The behaviour fails safe, so nothing is broken; but a user running `dcgB` who updates the module is silently returned to `stock` while the installer's own text tells them their mode was preserved. That is the same class as `FIND-2.2-03` and `FIND-1.5-01`'s original form — a promise in prose with no code behind it — now demonstrated end to end on a real upgrade rather than inferred.

**Two post-flash observations that look like defects and are not.** The tile application reports `versionCode=11` against the module's `versionCode=12`, and `lastUpdateTime=2026-08-22`, four days before the flash. Both resolve: `ishtar-cam-modes/tile-app/AndroidManifest.xml` itself declares `versionCode="11" versionName="2.1"`, so the tile application keeps its own version space and 11 is the correct value; and the installed `base.apk` md5 is `c5dfd5213f43c130174cd7ae01f876ed`, **byte-identical** to the `tile-app.apk` this flash wrote into the module directory. The bytes already matched, so no reinstall was triggered and the stale install date is expected. The v2.1 launcher fix is therefore the code actually running, notwithstanding the date. Recorded because a later session reading either field in isolation would reasonably suspect a partial install.

**`F2.2-07` — the baseline is named `BASELINE-2026-08-31-V21`**, and it is `snapshots/20260831-1607__faz2.2__post-flash-v2.1-baseline.txt`: module v2.1 / versionCode 12, no persisted mode, sensor and HAL both stock with one bind mount each, `camera.hal0` at 440 stream-configuration rows with the two `4096` entries at format `37`. Phases 3 through 5 diff against this identifier. Note for those phases that the baseline is a **stock-mode** state, not the `dcgB` state earlier sub-plans were captured against.

**`F2.2-08` — the flash date is 2026-08-31**, install completing at 19:03 local (16:03 UTC) and the post-flash bracket taken at 16:08 UTC. `EV-14` is closed by measurement.

**A contributing cause of the lost transcript, worth one line.** `.gitignore:48` ignores `*.log`, so `install.log` at the workspace root is invisible to `git status` and would never be committed by any ordinary `git add -A`. The IMPL-11 transcript is therefore preserved as `snapshots/20260831-1603__faz2.2__install-transcript-v2.1.txt` under the smoke-bracket naming convention, which is version controlled. Installer transcripts are device-event records and belong with the brackets rather than at the root under an extension the repository discards.

**`FIND-2.2-01` remains predicted, not measured, and this run could not change that.** The install was performed from `stock` mode precisely so the guard would pass, and it did pass — `customize.sh:23-27` was satisfied with `9e4d0fe1a8bf2eb4146c9917a0ac2c4c` on both sides. That is a positive control for the guard's pass path and confirms the workaround works, but it cannot demonstrate the abort. The original aborted transcript was not retained. The finding stays open at P2 and routed to Phase 9 on the strength of the source reading in IMPL-09.

**`F2.2-06` closes Faz 2.2, and the launcher fix is verified.** The user confirms the app-drawer icon renders correctly rather than appearing blank, which is the one v2.1 claim no host-side check could settle — `AUTOPSY-P1-02` predicted the shipped artifact might differ from the validated tree, and on its headline change it does not. All four QuickSettings tiles work. The camera opens and captures. Every acceptance criterion in section 8 of the sub-plan is met.

**Two user observations, both diagnosed as planned work rather than regressions.**

*"With 50MP enabled, AGC still outputs 12MP."* Not a Faz 2.2 defect, and not yet valid evidence of a Phase 3 failure, for two independent reasons — both already on the roadmap. First, `prefs.trap.count = 2` in every snapshot in this run: `pref_fix_resolution_key` and `pref_raw_key` are present in the installed AGC's preferences, and `README.md` states that a pinned preference overrides what the HAL advertises, so a measurement taken now measures the preference and not the HAL. Clearing them is `Faz 3.1`, which exists precisely to prevent this reading. Second, and more informative: **the HAL layer is working.** The fullres HAL adds seven `8192 6144` stream-configuration rows to `camera.hal0` — format `33` OUTPUT, and formats `34`, `35` and `54` as both INPUT and OUTPUT — so 50MP *is* advertised. But `pixelArray` stays `[4096 3072]` in both states, and 4096x3072 is 12.6 MP, exactly the size the user received. That is the documented Gcam wall: the consumer requires `pixelArraySize` to match the maximum advertised output, and while it does not, the 8192 entries are eliminated in `getOutputSizes`. Locating and moving that write site is `Faz 3.2`. The observation therefore **confirms the project's central hypothesis rather than contradicting it**, and it should be carried into Phase 3 as supporting evidence.

*"DCG and LN2/LN4 cannot be used simultaneously."* Correct, by construction, and combining them would not help. The sensor layer is one blob at a single bind-mounted path and `$MODDIR/mode` holds one value, so the modes are alternatives by design, not stackable features. `ishtar-cam-modes/Kullanim.txt` shows they are competing solutions to the same problem and that the comparison is already settled: line 18 marks `dcgB` `>>> ONERILEN <<<` (recommended); line 28 describes `ln4f` as applying LN4 plus 12-bit with quality equal to `dcgB`; lines 29-30 record that it produces more motion blur and that `dcgB` is to be preferred; line 31 calls `ln2f` the least advantageous; and lines 33-34 record `ln2s`/`ln4s` as measured to give no gain and to be noisier than stock. The exclusion that *is* potentially removable is the different one — `50mp` versus the sensor modes — because those live in two layers, HAL and sensor, that the module currently forces apart at `post-fs-data.sh:16-18`. That is `Faz 5.3`'s subject, with `Faz 5.2` supplying the composition test.

**`OQ-17` is answered as a free by-product, and the answer is not the one the question's phrasing anticipated.** The question asks which HAL camera id the fullres patch modifies, so that `Faz 4.1` can state its array count. Diffing the `fullres` snapshot against the baseline shows the patch modifies **every camera id on the device, all thirteen** — not one. The per-id stream-configuration deltas are: `hal0` 440 -> 456 (+16); `hal1` 372 -> 452 (+80); `hal8` and `hal9` 432 -> 508 (+76 each); `hal2` through `hal7` and `hal10` 500 -> 508 (+8 each); `hal11` and `hal12` 120 -> 124 (+4 each). Front-facing ids move too, which no prior document anticipated. `Faz 4.1` must therefore size its guard against a patch with device-wide reach rather than a single-id one. **The `OQ-17` row in `Planner-docs/Project-Ontology.md` is deliberately left unedited**, since that file is outside this sub-plan's section 9 boundary; `Faz 4.1` should update it from this entry. This is the same boundary situation that left `AUDIT-FIX-04` stale after `Faz 1.6`, and it is flagged here rather than silently repeated.

**Device state at the close of Faz 2.2 is not the baseline.** The user left the device in `50mp` with the fullres HAL mounted. `BASELINE-2026-08-31-V21` remains the named reference; `Faz 2.3` should either return the device to it or record its starting state explicitly.

| IMPL-14 | 2026-08-31 | `Planner-docs/Faz-5-Plans/Faz5.1-twelve-bit-mode-zero-blob.md` | F5.1-01 through F5.1-07, the complete sub-phase, plus the ontology backlog that three previous sessions could not reach. Host-side only; **no device interaction occurred**, which is itself acceptance criterion 9. | **`VAL-BASELINE`**: `python tools/verify_ln.py` passes against `stock.bin` and all eight variants before the candidate was judged; `parse()` asserts header, `0x1C` size field and CRC32 on every load. **`VAL-BLOB`**: candidate passes all three integrity checks. **`VAL-SIZE`**: exactly **1,062,250 bytes**. **`VAL-DIFF`**: 8 byte ranges, 16 bytes, every range named and attributed to an owning table entry; per-mode snapshot shows one mode changed `[0]` and 22 identical; the full comparison across every `regSetting` register of all 23 modes, the whole 304-byte `resolutionData` record and the whole `streamConfiguration` entry confirms **only mode 0 differs**, with `sensorDriverData` byte-identical; entries #48/#51/#57/#60 are referenced by mode 0 alone, so the offset redirections cannot leak into another mode; decomposition against `stock` isolates the addition to exactly `{0}` (84 ranges to 91, 7 added). Regeneration is byte-identical. **`VAL-DOCS`**: `docs/mode0-12bit-candidate.md`; ontology section 3 carries the container layout, mode-table field map and measured bit-depth encodings. | Faz 5.1 **complete**, all nine acceptance criteria met. The candidate is deliberately **outside `blobs/`**. One finding raised, `FIND-5.1-01`. Next is Faz 5.2, which needs explicit user approval. |

**The bit-depth encodings were read, not assumed** (acceptance criterion 2). 10-bit is `0x0112`/`0x0113` = `0x0A` with stream `dt` `0x2B` / `bpp` 10; 12-bit is `0x0C` with `dt` `0x2C` / `bpp` 12. Read from `stock` mode 0 and `dcgB` modes 1 and 14.

**`FIND-5.1-01` (P2, raised and handled here).** `ln_multimode.apply_ln()` clamps frame length to `FLL_MIN = 3200`. Every mode in `FOTO_MODLARI` is at most 3,072 lines, so the clamp is safe for everything it was written for. **Mode 0 is 6,144 lines**, so reusing that path unmodified would have set a frame length shorter than the frame itself — producing a blob that passes every integrity check while being wrong. `tools/mode0_12bit.py` leaves field 13 untouched and guards the condition explicitly. This is the substantive answer to F5.1-03: the bit-depth change does imply a second field must move (`llp`, scaled 12/10 for MIPI, recorded as device-verified at `ln_multimode.py:196-199`), and one field that must **not**.

**A recorded deviation: the candidate is generated from `dcgB.bin`, not `stock.bin`.** `Faz5.1` section 9 names `stock.bin` as the differential reference, and it is used as one — `VAL-BASELINE` ran against it and the section 6c decomposition is taken against it. The generation base is `dcgB` because white and black levels are **global, not per-mode**: a `stock` + mode-0-12-bit blob would put a 12-bit mode 0 under a 1023 white level, which is internally incoherent and contains no DCG layer, so mounting it with the `50mp` HAL would test nothing that plain `stock` + `50mp` does not. `dcgB` is the recommended variant and the one whose global level creates the exclusion. Reasoning recorded in `docs/mode0-12bit-candidate.md` section 2 rather than left implicit.

**Ontology backlog cleared.** `Faz5.1`'s section 9 boundary is the first in three sessions to include `Planner-docs/Project-Ontology.md`, so the items IMPL-12 and IMPL-13 had to defer are now written: `OQ-17` moved to `partially_answered` with the thirteen-camera-id delta table in section 3 and the explicit note that Faz 4.1 cannot state a single target array count; invariants 3 and 4 marked as holding under direct test, with invariant 4 additionally carrying the reason `50mp` + LN has never been reachable; invariant 5 marked **unassessable** rather than confirmed or refuted. `AUDIT-FIX-04` in `Sub-Planing-Audit.md` remains deferred — that file is **not** in this sub-plan's boundary and was not touched.

**A defect in this ledger, flagged rather than fixed.** The `Faz2.3-ten-mode-regression-pass.md` row in section 4 carries four trailing cells beyond the nine-column header (`| 2026-08-31 | regression record. | - | 2026-08-24 |`), apparently a fragment of another row. The validator does not detect it. It is left unedited because correcting another sub-plan's recorded evidence row without that sub-plan's context is the opportunistic edit this project has repeatedly chosen not to make; route it to `Faz 9.3` with the other documentation-consolidation items.

The composition works.** `OQ-04` answered, `H-04` raised to `probable`, invariant 4 rewritten as a removable constraint. Faz 5.3 now has its evidence. One finding raised, `FIND-5.2-01`. |

**The headline result: the two layers are not physically exclusive.** `dcgB` with mode 0 at 12-bit ran under the `fullres` HAL, the camera opened without a Gcam NPE, preview was live in two applications, and OpenCamera produced a valid 50.3 MP frame. **The historical corruption symptom did not reproduce** — no green cast, no pink banding, no torn or repeated rows. Nothing beyond the bit-depth mismatch had to move, which is exactly what `Kararlar #4` predicted and what `OQ-04` asked.

**Three limits on that claim, stated so Faz 5.3 does not over-read it.** First, the sensor layer tested was **DCG**, not LN: `ln2`/`ln4` crash AGC on their own (`FIND-2.3-02`), so composing them would confound the result, and `H-04` is therefore `probable` rather than `confirmed`. Second, the composed capture is **visually indistinguishable** from the same shot at `50mp` alone — same exposure, tone and noise, file sizes 2,669,862 vs 2,674,023 bytes, 0.16% apart. The composition is *reachable*; it is not yet shown to be *better*, and the test scene was dark and flat, which is a weak discriminator for noise and detail. Third, only the photo path was exercised deliberately; the video result below was incidental.

**Method note — the mount was a content rewrite, not a second bind mount.** `setmode.sh:251` changes the contents of the already-mounted inode `$MODDIR/active/$BIN` and leaves the mount alone. The test helper reproduced that, plus the `EXPECT_SIZE` guard, the post-write md5 verification, and the client-before-HAL restart ordering from `setmode.sh:47-91`. Consequences worth keeping: mount count never left 1, restoration was a content rewrite through the module's own `setmode.sh stock`, and because `mode.file` still read `50mp` throughout, **a reboot alone would have re-asserted the stock blob** — the experiment self-heals.

**Incidental result: video recorded under composition.** A mis-sent `KEYCODE_CAMERA` started an OpenCamera recording, which ran 22 seconds and saved an 8.1 MB `VID_20260831_221602.mp4` before being stopped. Unintended, but it is direct evidence that the video path functions with both layers active. It does **not** bear on invariant 5, which is about 1080p60/4K60 through the profiles that remain unreachable, nor on `FIND-2.3-03`, which is AGC's video section specifically. The file was left on the device rather than deleted, since it is evidence.

**`FIND-5.2-01` (P2, open) — `scripts/smoke.sh`'s `prefs.trap.*` rows are not reproducible, and were the only pre/post difference.** At 19:06 the pre-test snapshot reported `prefs.trap.count = 2` with keys `pref_fix_resolution_key pref_raw_key`. Minutes later, a direct `grep -c` of both files in `shared_prefs/` returned **0** for each key, and `org.codeaurora.snapcam_preferences.xml` measured **0 bytes**. At 19:20 smoke.sh itself reported `count = 0`, and after AGC rewrote its preferences to 5,851 bytes a direct grep still returned 0 for both. Two independent methods now agree the trap is **not armed**. Two details argue the `2` was a false positive rather than a real reading: the reported keys carry **no `_N` lens suffix**, which real matches would (invariant 8 describes `pref_raw_key_N`), and the file was empty when they were reported — they look like the two literal fragments of the `-E` alternation pattern, whose `|` must survive several layers of `sh_root` quoting. Not proven, and the `0` at 19:20 does not fit that theory cleanly. **Consequence: a single smoke reading of these two rows must not be trusted as the invariant-8 precondition.** Routed to `Faz 3.1`, which owns preference-trap clearance and the per-measurement checklist `FIND-2.3-01` already made mandatory. AGC showing its first-run dialog during the test independently corroborates that its preferences had been wiped at some point before this run.

**The gate earned its place: precondition 2 failed.** `Faz 3.2` searches a 27 MB stripped binary over nine `movz`/`movk` references and six rodata dwords, which needs real disassembly. `capstone` and `pyelftools` are **not installed** — `docs/reverse-engineering-toolchain.md:24-25` already said so and it was confirmed by import. The four-site self-check still passed because a *known* encoding can be compared byte-for-byte without a disassembler, which is exactly the distinction the precondition exists to expose: the check passing does not mean the capability exists. **`FIND-3.1-01` (P2, open): install `capstone` and `pyelftools` before `Faz 3.2`.** Recorded rather than performed, since `Faz3.1` section 5 places all disassembly work in `Faz 3.2`.

**The address book is intact against the current vendor build.** `0x16C18DC` = `cmp w11,#2`, `0x16C1C18` = `cmp w7,#2`, `0x16C22BC` and `0x16C2330` = `cmp w20,#2`, and `0x16C2334` = `b.lo` — confirming the ontology's own warning that the fifth address is the branch, not a fifth patch site. Worth having: it means no OTA has moved the offsets since they were derived.

**`FIND-3.1-02` (P3, open) — the sub-plan's ninth acceptance criterion is stale.** It expects the baseline to show neither the experimental HAL patch nor the patched APK applied. The HAL half holds. The APK half does not: the **patched** AGC `ce65f862c990e2acc02be356f75a7122` has been installed since before `IMPL-12`, and `README.md` has recorded it all along — the expectation was inherited from Autopsy section 4 and never updated. **User decision, 2026-08-31: keep the patched build.** Phase 3 measures against a patched AGC, and checklist item 7 now requires every result to name the build it was measured against. The consequence carried forward is that `FIND-2.3-03`'s cheapest control — installing stock `AGC8.4.300_V9.6_snap.apk` (`fa4066eb3bf82cc097906fd108073500`) — is deliberately not taken yet.

**Attempt budget, agreed with the user before any search begins, per `AUTOPSY-P1-03`: 15 candidate sites across at most 2 implementation sessions.** The 15 are exactly the addresses already in the ontology address book for tag `f0006`. The bound is exhaustive over that recorded set, which is what makes it a real bound: it either locates the write site or **proves the recorded set does not contain it**, and the second outcome would redirect the search rather than extend it. On exhaustion `Faz 3.6` runs as a scheduled gate, not at the implementer's discretion.

**Three fallback options are now defined with cost and risk**, so `Faz 3.6` chooses rather than improvises: (A) accept the application-level smali patch, cheapest but app-specific and leaves the `libgcam` wall standing; (B) move the active array instead of the pixel array — **not a cheaper path**, since invariant 6 requires both sides to move together and the Faz 4 attempt already failed from that direction; (C) defer Phase 3. Option C is materially stronger than it was this morning: `IMPL-15` proved the device captures 50.3 MP through OpenCamera with both layers composed, so the *capability* is demonstrated and what Phase 3 buys is that capability **inside AGC specifically**.

**On the preference trap itself, the useful new fact is a negative one.** AGC does not write the trap keys on startup, so the trap is a residue of manual configuration or a `.agc` import — and both root `.agc` files carry all twelve key names (12/12 in each, verified by name). That makes the import path the only realistic re-arming route, which is why the warning now sits in `README.md`'s artifact manifest, where an operator looks before using a root-level file.

**Correction to `FIND-5.2-01`'s stated cause.** `IMPL-15` suggested smoke.sh's `prefs.trap.*` rows echoed the alternation pattern's fragments. Reading `scripts/smoke.sh:49` refutes that: `sh_root` wraps the probe block in **single quotes**, so the `-E` alternation reaches the device intact and the grep is well-formed. The `count = 2` at 19:06 was therefore most likely a **real** reading of a preferences file that has since been replaced, not a detector fault — which means the trap was armed as recently as that evening and was cleared incidentally rather than deliberately. The finding stays open with its cause unestablished; the checklist's response is unchanged and now better justified: trust the per-key audit, use the smoke field as a cross-check, and record any disagreement.
