No sub-plan work was touched and no device was involved. |

**The disposition, and why it is not simply "it worked, so ship it."** `Faz5.3` section 11 anticipates exactly this case: "the composition works but with a quality cost that makes it undesirable — record the disposition as a decision informed by Faz 5.2's quality comparison, not as an automatic consequence of the mechanism working." Three measured reasons, put to the user, who chose to retain:
1. **No demonstrated benefit.** The composed 50.3 MP frame is visually indistinguishable from the same shot at `50mp` alone; the JPEGs differ by 0.16%.
2. **The named combinations cannot be validated.** `Faz5.3` scope names `50mp`+`ln2` and `50mp`+`ln4`. Both crash AGC on their own (`FIND-2.3-02`), so neither can produce a clean verdict. What was proven is `50mp`+**DCG**.
3. **Removal is not a dispatch edit.** See the inventory below.

**The enforcement inventory — six surfaces, not four, and only two of them are logic.**

| # | Surface | Site | Mechanism | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Boot dispatch | `post-fs-data.sh:15-27` | `50mp` -> `SENSOR_MODE=stock`; the nine sensor modes -> `HAL_WANT=stock` | **forcing logic** |
| 2 | Interactive dispatch | `setmode.sh:177-192` | structurally identical case | **forcing logic** |
| 3 | Effective-state reporting | `action.sh:94-104` `active()` | HAL-first: if `hal_state` is `fullres` it returns `50mp` and **never reads the sensor layer** | **state model** |
| 4 | Tile UI | `BaseTile.java:119,301` | `predicted = wasOn ? "stock" : mode()`; `on = current.equals(mode())` | **assumption, no rule** |
| 5 | WebUI | `webroot/index.html:187,260,317`, text at 149/177 | `MAIN_MODES`, `on = dataset.mode === current`, toggle -> `stock` | **assumption + text** |
| 6 | Installer text | `customize.sh:64` | `ui_print` describing the exclusion | **text** |

**`TRACE-06` is closed, and the answer inverts the risk `Faz5.3` section 11 named.** That risk was that the tile application enforces exclusivity in Java and would be missed. It does not: **the tile app contains no exclusion rule at all.** Tile exclusivity is *emergent* from the single-valued `$MODDIR/mode` string. That is worse news than a coded rule, not better — a coded rule could be deleted, whereas an emergent one means the whole state model assumes single-valuedness end to end: the `mode` file holds one string, `active()` returns one string, and both UIs render "on" by string equality.

**The concrete consequence, which is the real reason to retain.** Removing the two forcing rules is about six lines. Under the resulting composed state — `fullres` HAL plus a DCG sensor blob — `action.sh` `active()` would hit its HAL-first branch and report **`50mp`**, never inspecting the sensor layer; the DCG tile would compare `current.equals("dcgB")`, get false, and show **off** while DCG was actually running. Had the sensor blob been the candidate, it would match none of the nine variants and fall through to **`unknown`**. So a six-line edit does not deliver the capability — it delivers a capability the system **misreports**, which is the intermittent, hard-to-diagnose class of failure this project has already paid for once. Doing it properly means moving from one mode string to two independent layer states across `action.sh`, `BaseTile.java` and `webroot/index.html`.

**What is preserved so adoption stays cheap.** `ishtar-cam-modes/candidates/dcgB-m0-12bit.bin` (md5 `80b53215356c57b156f2bb2918cf067a`) and its generator `tools/mode0_12bit.py` are kept, with the full generation and composition record in `docs/mode0-12bit-candidate.md`. The candidate is **not** retired — this was not a failure — but it stays outside `blobs/` so no mode switch can select it. **Re-open invariant 4 only on new evidence of *benefit*; the mechanism question is settled.**

**What Phase 9 inherits: nothing new.** Because the disposition is retain, the release contents are unchanged — no new blob, no dispatch change, no changed capability claim. `Faz5.3` section 10 predicted that a success-path disposition would block Phase 9 on materially changed contents; it does not.

### IMPL-18 — Faz 3.2, the pixelArraySize write site (2026-09-01)

**The write site is located.** Entirely static: no device interaction, no patch, no capture, exactly as `Faz3.2` scope requires. Analysis ran against the stock HAL `c3fa63a86ee3ecaf9fbf02005d35f6b5` / 27,268,688 B — the same binary IMPL-16 verified — recovered from a prior session's working copy and re-checksummed before use, so no device pull was needed.

**The blocker is gone.** `pip install capstone pyelftools` on CPython 3.14.7 installed `capstone` 5.0.9 and `pyelftools` 0.33. `FIND-3.1-01` is closed. The 12 files carried into this session were committed first as `e19b8cf`, which also removes the one condition on `Faz3.1` precondition P1.

**Calibration passed 5/5 before anything else was trusted.** The four `cmp wN,#2` sites and the `0x16c2334` `b.lo` decode exactly as the address book records. The ELF layout added a fact worth keeping: file offset **equals** virtual address for `.text` and `.rodata`, so every recorded address is unambiguous.

**Half the address book for tag `f0006` was wrong, and that is the expensive half.** All **nine** recorded `movz`/`movk` sites are false positives — none of them builds `0xf0006`. Six load division magic constants (`0xffe56cb1`, `0xf83f630a`), one builds a 64-bit literal for a `stp`, and two load hashed keys. An exhaustive `.text` scan for the only encoding that can build the tag (`movz #6` + `movk #0xf, lsl #16`) finds **three** genuine sites, none of them among the nine. The six rodata dwords are the opposite case: genuine and provably exhaustive — they are the only six 4-byte-aligned `0x000f0006` dwords in the whole file — but **none is a value source**. They sit inside sequential tag-list runs or packed tuning data, and resolving every `adrp`+`add` in `.text` yields zero exact references to any of them.

**`H-01` is therefore contradicted, on both of its clauses.** The value is not written from a rodata dword, and nothing is reachable from the nine sites.

**What actually happens.** Inside the function starting `0x01661f40` (stripped; no symbol covers it), the HAL publishes the whole `android.sensor.info.*` family in sequence through the CamX metadata publisher at `0x1755430`. For tag `f0006` it loads sensor-data base `S = [x24+0x60]`, forms `S+0x3c1c`, reads selector `[S+0x3d84]` — the same field the address book already records with 55 accesses binary-wide — and copies the 8-byte `(width, height)` pair into the staging slot `[x19+0x6550]`, from `S+0x3c1c` or from `S+0x3db4` when the selector is 1. It then publishes that slot as tag `f0006` with **count 2** via `bl` at `0x0166648c`. Siblings in the same function corroborate the shape: `f0005` count 2 from `S+0x3dac`, `f000a` count 4 from `S+0x3d88`.

**F3.2-04 has a decisive answer: the value is runtime-derived, not a constant in the binary.** That is the fact `Faz3.2` flagged as potentially making a static patch infeasible. It does not — but it does determine the patch strategy, because there is no constant to edit.

**Ranked candidates handed to Faz 3.3.** (1) `0x01666440`, `mov w5, #0x3c1c` — one word whose immediate selects which pair in `S` is published; a genuine single-word change, conditional on confirming `S`'s layout on device. (2) `0x0166645c`, `ldr x1, [x4, #0x198]` — one word, but only on the selector-is-1 path. (3) Overwriting the load/store block with immediate construction of `8192x6144`; multi-word, alters control flow, ranked last.

**Budget: 3 of 15 sites consumed, 12 remain.** The search succeeded within budget, so `Faz 3.6` is **not** triggered as a scheduled gate.

**Recording discipline held.** Every address was written into `Project-Ontology.md` section 6 during the session, per `R10` and `QAS-03`, not deferred to a closing step. `OQ-01` answered, `CQ-05` `partially_answered` — its criterion demands a `dumpsys` change, which is `Faz3.3`'s to produce — `H-01` contradicted, and `EV-24`, `EV-25`, `EV-26` added.

**`FIND-3.2-01` (P2, open) — nine recorded addresses were wrong, and the method that produced them is unknown.** The nine `movz`/`movk` sites entered the address book before any ClaudeQB run and were carried forward through several handoffs as established fact, including into the 15-site attempt budget. They do not survive a decode. Their distribution is the tell: six of the nine are two *specific* division magic constants repeated across unrelated functions, which is the signature of a byte-pattern or substring search rather than an instruction decode. **The finding is not the nine wrong addresses — it is that other entries in the address book may share that provenance.** The four `cmp` sites are safe: they have now been decode-verified twice (IMPL-16, IMPL-18). What has *not* been re-derived by decode should be treated as unvalidated until it is. Routed to `Faz 9.3` for a documentation sweep and to `docs/reverse-engineering-toolchain.md`, whose `VAL-DISASM` self-check is exactly the control that catches this class of error. Cost of the miss, measured: zero patches applied to a wrong address, because `Faz3.2` calibrated before it searched.

### IMPL-19 — Faz 9.2 recovery runbook, pulled forward (2026-09-01)

**Why it ran out of order.** The Step 3 audit's single most important remediation action: the master plan's risk `R3` asks for a recovery path, `Faz2.1` was made read-only by design, and the only written runbook sat in `Faz9.2` at index position 13 — behind three phases that patch a system binary on a daily-driver device. Presented to the user as a fork before `Faz3.3`; they chose to pull it forward. Only the runbook half moved (F9.2-05, F9.2-06, F9.2-07); the checklist half stays in Phase 9, where it genuinely belongs, because it depends on `Faz9.3`'s corrected claims, `Faz9.1`'s build script, and a device.

**The central finding was verified, not assumed, and it is better news than expected.** A mechanical audit of every write in every module script — `dd of=`, `cat >`, `cp`, `mv`, `rm`, `chcon`, `chmod`, `chown` — shows all writes land in `$MODDIR/active/` or `$MODDIR/active_hal/`, both module-private. All ten `/odm` references are `mount --bind` targets, `md5sum` reads, the single `cat` read source in `hal_seed`, or `/proc/1/mountinfo` greps. **There is no write to `/odm` anywhere in the module.** Since a bind mount does not survive a reboot, `touch …/disable` plus a restart is a complete restore for every failure this module can cause — no reflash, no `fastboot`, no data loss. Ontology invariant 1 holds mechanically rather than by intention, and `CQ-09`'s first half answers **no**.

**Eight guards tabulated, and their shape matters.** Every one fails closed and ends in `exit 0` rather than aborting boot: a tripped guard costs the feature, not the phone. That is why `Faz9.2`'s boot-failure scenario is short — the module is an unlikely cause of a boot loop, and the runbook says so rather than padding.

**Four residual failure modes, one of them new.** `FIND-9.2-01` (P3, latent, no occurrence observed): `chown`, `chmod` and `chcon` are all `2>/dev/null` silenced in all three places they appear — `post-fs-data.sh:68-70`, `halpatch.sh:112-114`, `setmode.sh:253-255`. If `chcon u:object_r:vendor_file:s0` fails, the bind mount still succeeds and every md5 guard still passes, but `cameraserver` cannot load the library. **It presents as "camera will not open" with every checksum correct** — the most confusing signature in the runbook, which is exactly why it is worth having written down before it happens. Routed to `Faz9.1`, which owns packaging assertions. The other three residuals: stacked mounts giving a false "restored to stock" reading (`R1`, the reason `hal_umount_all` loops to 40), a byte-valid but semantically wrong patch that passes identity checks (`R2` — `QAS-01`'s scenario, and the one most relevant to `Faz3.3`), and the case where the module simply is not the cause (`R4`, per `FIND-2.3-03`).

**Honest limit, recorded in the document itself.** `ARC-11` moves to **documented, not demonstrated**. No step has been executed against a deliberately broken device; that is F9.2-04's known-good/known-bad test and it stays in Phase 9. The runbook is a verified reading of the source, not a rehearsed procedure, and it says so in its own section 10.

### IMPL-20 — Faz 3.3, the write site confirmed by measurement (2026-09-01)

**The result in one line: `pixelArraySize` now moves, on one word, reversibly — and the value the project spent two phases hunting was already sitting in the register it needed to be in.**

**The design question was answered statically, which is why this cost one attempt instead of several.** The handoff flagged an open question — nobody had established which offset in the sensor structure `S` holds `8192x6144` — and suggested tracing `InitializeScalerStaticCaps` to find out. The trace went one level further back, to the structure's *initializer* rather than its readers, which turned out to be decisive. Scanning `.text` for 32-bit accesses at the known sibling offsets found exactly one write region, `0x016f0330`…`0x016f0830`, and it states the relation in the clear: `[S+0x3db4] = [S+0x3c1c] / [x21+0x160]` and `[S+0x3db8] = [S+0x3c20] / [S+0x3d80]`, with the binning factor computed as `(int)sqrt([x27+8])` — `sqrt(4) = 2` for a 2x2 bin. `S+0x3c1c` is therefore the **unbinned** pixel array and `S+0x3db4` the **binned** one, and the displacement between them, `0x198`, is exactly candidate 2's immediate.

**That inverted the candidate ranking.** Candidate 1 (`0x01666440`, `mov w5, #0x3c1c`) was **withdrawn without being spent**: its immediate already points at the unbinned pair, so editing it moves away from the answer. Candidate 2, previously ranked second because "its effect is conditional", became the determinate choice — and the condition resolved itself. The reported `f0006` was `[4096 3072]`, which *is* the binned pair; had the selector been anything but `1`, the publisher's default path would already have been publishing `S+0x3c1c`. So the selector was `1`, `S+0x3c1c` already held `8192x6144`, and the value was being loaded into `x17` at `0x0166644c` on every single call and then immediately overwritten.

**A warning in the handoff was also refined.** "A patch must satisfy BOTH publication paths" is true of the *publications* but not of the *value write*: the second path (`0x016664b0`…`0x016664c8`) rebuilds the same tag and count and hands the trampoline at `0x1662098` the **same** `[x19+0x6550]` staging slot, deriving nothing of its own. Patching the value write satisfies both by construction — and the measurement confirmed it.

**Two free self-checks were run before touching the device, and both paid.** Replaying the sixteen shipped `hal-fullres.patch` entries onto stock reproduced `fe0245a4720fb07b01d96deef47f5569` byte-exactly, which validated the four-byte index convention and the octal-escape decoding against a known-good answer before any of it was trusted on a new table. It also established what the fullres patch *is*: all sixteen sites are `ldr wN,[xM,#0x3d84]` -> `movz wN,#0`, i.e. force the selector to zero throughout `InitializeScalerStaticCaps`, which is why it emits the whole sensor mode table and touches all thirteen camera ids. Second, both resulting md5s were computed and **written into the patch table before the run**, so each write tested a prediction rather than recorded an outcome. Both matched.

**The third-md5 trap was handled by design, not survived by luck.** An experimental patch yields an md5 `hal_state` does not recognize, and `post-fs-data.sh` answers `unknown` by re-seeding from stock. Two consequences were planned for: the measurement was taken **without rebooting**, and `hal_set` was deliberately *not* used — the module's own reconciliation would have undone the experiment mid-run. The write instead mirrored `restart_camera`'s client-before-HAL ordering (invariant 3) in a temporary script, substituting a single `dd` for `hal_set`, and rewrote the contents of the already-mounted inode exactly as `setmode.sh:251` does for the sensor. Mount count stayed `1` throughout, read as a *count* per `R1` rather than inferred from a `umount` exit status. Against `FIND-9.2-01`, `chown`/`chmod`/`chcon` were checked **explicitly** rather than `2>/dev/null` silenced, and the SELinux context was verified as `u:object_r:vendor_file:s0` after each write.

**Validation was complete and none of it was asserted.** Three smoke runs bracketed the attempt; the comparable region of the third is byte-identical to the first. The three array tags were read in all three runs and kept distinct — `f0006` moved while `f0000` and `f000a` provably did not, which is the check that would have caught a patch landing on the wrong array. The user confirmed a live preview in the stock camera; the crash buffer was empty and no `ishtar_cam_modes` `DURDU:` line appeared. The preference trap audited 0/12 by the per-key method, and the `smoke.sh` field **agreed** this run — recorded as a `FIND-5.2-01` cross-check rather than as a resolution.

**What this does not establish.** `H-02` is held at `tentative` on purpose. Raising the tag is proven; *libgcam accepting it* is not, and that is Faz 3.5's evidence, exactly as the sub-plan's own risk register anticipated ("the metadata changes but libgcam still rejects it"). One stock camera opening is one consumer, not the survey Faz 3.4 owes. `R2` from the recovery runbook remains live in principle — the guards verify identity, not correctness — which is why the reversal was demonstrated rather than assumed.

**Budget and state.** One attempt consumed: **4 of 15 candidate sites, 1 of 2 implementation sessions**. `Faz3.6` is not triggered. The device is back at `BASELINE-2026-08-31-V21` on every layer value, the temporary script is deleted, and `Faz3.4` and `Faz3.5` are unblocked.

### IMPL-22 — Faz 3.4, the consumer survey (2026-09-01)

**The result in one line: the tag can be raised to 8192x6144 without breaking any consumer that was tested — including the 50 MP capture path itself, which is the one that mattered most.**

**The headline is row 3 read together with row 8.** With the fullres HAL and the experimental word both in place, `camera.hal0` reported `streamcfg=456` **and** `pixelArray=[8192 6144]` at the same time — the first time `frame_raw_max` and `pixelArraySize` have ever both read `8192x6144` on this device, which is precisely the state Project-Ontology invariant 6 describes. Open Camera then captured a clean `8192x6144` = 50.3 MP frame from that state, so `EV-04` survives the change rather than merely coexisting with it. Whether **libgcam** accepts the same state is Faz 3.5's question and is deliberately unanswered here; no AGC interaction occurred, which is one of this sub-plan's acceptance criteria.

**Measurement discipline.** Every dimension came from the artifact, not from a label: JPEG sizes from the SOF marker, video from the MP4 container. That mattered — the container confirmed the two profiles really were `1920x1080 @ 60.1 fps` and `3840x2160 @ 60.0 fps` rather than whatever the UI claimed. Row 5 (4K60) had **no clean prior reading at all** in `docs/regression-v2.1.md`; it now has one.

**The framing row was rescued by ordering, not by luck.** `IMPL-21` flagged that a fixed-position capture had to be taken *before* the patch went back on, or `F3.4-06` could only ever return "not assessable". That capture was taken first, the phone was propped and never moved across the pair, and the comparison is therefore genuinely controlled: shelf edge, skirting line, plank seams and cable all land at the same coordinates, with only auto-exposure differing. No field-of-view change.

**One of this file's own earlier claims was corrected.** `IMPL-21` recorded row 3's expectation as a flat 50.3 MP citing `EV-04`. That was imprecise: `EV-04` was measured under the **fullres** HAL. At stock HAL, Open Camera captures `4096x3072` both before and after the change. The 50.3 MP figure is a property of the fullres HAL, not of Open Camera, and this is why the survey was run on **both** bases rather than one.

**Three findings, and none of them is the patch.** The most consequential is `FIND-3.4-01`: once `active_hal/` carries a third md5, **no runtime mode switch can move the HAL layer in either direction**, because `hal_state` returns `unknown`, that is the only path calling `hal_seed`, and `hal_seed` refuses while the target is bind-mounted. Observed twice, in both directions. The module warns loudly rather than failing silently, and the sensor layer still switches correctly, so the failure is a **state mismatch** — `mode` reading `50mp` while the HAL is not `fullres` — not corruption. The mechanism was confirmed rather than assumed: reverting the word to a *recognized* md5 made `hal_set` succeed immediately. The operational rule that follows, **revert the experimental word before any mode switch**, was used for the rest of the session and worked every time. This dissolves once Phase 4 consolidates the patch into a table whose result md5 is a recognized constant.

**A withdrawn conclusion, recorded because withdrawing it was the point.** When `dcgB` aborted the camera provider, the first disambiguation — reverting the word and seeing the crash persist — looked like clean evidence that the composition rather than the patch was at fault. It was not clean: `FIND-3.4-03` established that a provider crash **contaminates the entire boot**, so every reading after the first abort showed the same `CSLAcquireHardwareHW` signature in *any* mode, including plain `stock`. That intermediate conclusion was therefore withdrawn, and the question was re-answered on a fresh boot, where `dcgB` + stock HAL + **no word** still aborts while `dcgA` on the same boot is clean. `FIND-3.4-02` is consequently attributed to `dcgB` alone and explicitly **not** to Faz 3.3's change. Note that `docs/regression-v2.1.md` row 4 records `dcgB` as PASS, but that row tested **AGC**; these two consumers were never tested against `dcgB`, so this is a gap in coverage rather than a contradiction of the record.

**Two follow-ups were routed rather than performed**, following the discipline this project already applies to `AUDIT-FIX-04`. `FIND-3.4-03`'s addition to the `Faz 3.1` per-measurement checklist belongs in `docs/phase3-preconditions.md`, and `H-02`'s consumer conjunct now has strong evidence that belongs in `Project-Comprehension.md` — **neither file is in Faz 3.4's Implementation Contract**, so neither was edited. Both are routed to Faz 3.5, whose contract should cover them.

**State.** Device restored: the post-survey smoke snapshot's comparable region is byte-identical to the pre-survey one. Temporary device scripts removed. Three reboots occurred during the session, all deliberate and all recovering cleanly.

## 8. Open Decisions and Follow-Ups

| ID | Decision | Owner | Raised by | Blocks | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DEC-02 | The `tiles hide` option's disposition: repair, remove, or label as a known limitation. Removal is the recommended default because an absent control is better than one that silently does nothing. | User | Main-Planing prioritized step 10; `Planner-docs/Faz-2-Plans/Faz2.4-tiles-hide-disposition.md` | Nothing; it is the one Phase 2 item Phase 3 does not depend on | **Open, but now informed by `IMPL-71` (2026-09-06), and three of its premises have changed.** (1) **Repair is excluded on evidence**, not preference: the `pm disable` succeeds and AOSPA Launcher3 declines to honour it, so no module-side fix exists. (2) **Removal is no longer the obvious default.** The stated argument for it — “an absent control beats a lying one” — assumed a *visible* control; there is none. `tiles hide`/`show` are shell subcommands, `show` is the documented KernelSU root-grant recovery path (`Kullanim.txt:100-103`), and `hide` genuinely works on **other** launchers (`action.sh:80`). Removing them would delete a working feature on non-AOSPA launchers to fix a cosmetic mismatch on this one. **Follow-ups raised 2026-09-05 by planning run 13.**

- **`FIND-3.7-03` (new, P3) — the front camera's photo size re-derives too, and no Phase 3 matrix accounted for it.** `IMPL-54` measured `pref_camera_picturesize_front_key` moving `6528x4896` → `3264x2448` across the base transition, tracking the shipped base's front `pixelArray` of `[3264 2448]` against the experimental base's `[6528 4896]`. Nothing in Phase 3 asked about the front camera, so this is not a defect in any sub-plan; it is a gap in coverage. **Routed by this run:** ~~`Faz3.10` now reads the front key per axis~~, `Faz3.9`'s banner records that its rear-camera evidence does not cover a front-camera session, and `Faz3.11` disposes of it. Recorded as an ID so it can be tracked rather than living only inside an `IMPL-54` sentence. **Corrected 2026-09-05 by planning run 14 (`AUDIT-FIX-31`): the struck clause was not true.** The seventh Step 3 audit measured that `front` occurs in `Faz3.10` exactly once, in its section 1 banner, and in no scope bullet, work item or acceptance criterion — so the routing this row claimed does not exist in the file it points at, and `Faz3.11`'s `F3.11-08` was proposing to close the finding on it. `Faz3.11` is repaired (`F3.11-08` now verifies rather than assumes, and disposes of the finding itself if `Faz3.10` did not measure the key). ~~**`Faz3.10` is not repaired and the routing is therefore still open**: the pending amendment either adds the front-key read to `Faz3.10`'s executable sections or declares the front camera out of scope in its section 5, at which point this finding is disposed of in `Faz3.11`.~~ **Closed 2026-09-05 by planning run 15, which took the first branch.** `Faz3.10` now reads `pref_camera_picturesize_front_key` on every axis and on both halves of the negative control, in section 4, in `F3.10-02` through `F3.10-05`, in `F3.10-07`/`F3.10-08`, in `VAL-PREFPIN`, in an acceptance criterion and in a transition criterion — not only in a banner. The key is a **recorded value, not a fifth verdict axis**: no disturbance is added for it, and a movement in it is routed as a finding against this ID rather than failing the axis row. `Faz3.10` must state explicitly whether any axis moved it, which is the evidence `Faz3.11`'s `F3.11-08` requires in order to re-route or dispose of this finding against measurement rather than against a plan's intention.
- **Three of the eight audit findings are deliberately still open after this run, and the reasons differ.** `AUDIT-FIX-22` (the ontology `Escape` column's 7 control bytes) is independent of Phase 3 and equally bounded; it was left out only to keep this amendment's diff attributable to one cause, and the sixth audit supplies a ready prompt for it. `AUDIT-FIX-30` (`Main-Planing.md` section 6's second Phase 3 fact) is deferred to the close of Phase 3 alongside `AUDIT-FIX-20`, because editing the master plan is this section's replanning trigger and would supersede the active snapshot for one already-marked sentence. `AUDIT-FIX-25` is `accepted` and needs no action.
- **This run did not re-audit itself, and that is a gate rather than a note.** `Faz3.8` … `Faz3.11` must not re-enter the Step 4 queue until Step 3 has read these amendments. The reasoning is the sixth audit's own: a bounded amendment that changes a sub-plan's *field design* and its *negative-control stimulus* — not merely its wording — is exactly the kind of change an audit exists to check.

**Superseded follow-up, corrected here rather than left standing.** This section previously recorded that the tile application's Java sources had never been read and that the tile-exclusivity mechanism was therefore unknown. **IMPL-17 read them and closed `TRACE-06`:** the tile application contains no exclusion rule at all, and exclusivity is emergent from the single-valued mode string (`BaseTile.java:119,301` is the assumption `on = current.equals(mode())`, not a rule). The gap is closed, not outstanding.

**The claim Phase 9 inherits, written once here in the form `Faz9.2` and `Faz9.3` consume (`F3.11-09`).** The canonical copy is `docs/agc-12mp-configuration.md` section 10.

> On a Xiaomi 13 Ultra running the `50mp` module mode with the shipped `fullres` HAL `fe0245a4720fb07b01d96deef47f5569` and the stock sensor `9e4d0fe1a8bf2eb4146c9917a0ac2c4c`, Google Camera `ce65f862c990e2acc02be356f75a7122` configured with `pref_48m_key=0` and `pref_camera_resolution=full` captures a clean 12.58 MP photograph — black fraction below `0.01` by the `IMPL-40` method — and that configuration survived AGC cold start, camera-stack restart, QuickSettings mode switch and device reboot with zero field changes (`IMPL-61`, four axes, no failures).

**Proving command:** `scripts/smoke.sh -o <snapshot>` reporting `agc.pref_48m=0`, `agc.pref_resolution=full` and `agc.photo_size.back.derived=4096x3072`, followed by one capture measured under `0.01`.

**The claim is conditioned on what the matrix actually showed, per `Faz3.11`'s section 11 risk.** All four axes survived and **no axis failed**, so the claim carries no failure caveat — but its scope is narrow and is stated with it: rear camera, one device, 12.58 MP only. It says nothing about the front camera's images, about distribution readiness (`Faz4.4`), or about AGC at any other resolution. `Faz9.3` uses it to retire `EV-23`'s contradicted "AGC cannot exceed 15.2 MP" claim.

**The read-back is done and the claim is cleared to propagate.** `F3.11-10` was performed 2026-09-05 as `IMPL-63`: the document was followed from a genuinely unpinned state, every predicted field and both stream pairs matched, and the capture measured 12.58 MP at black `0.0001` against the `< 0.01` threshold. **One defect was found and corrected in the same session** — section 2's original write procedure truncated the live preferences file to 0 bytes when its `sed` step failed, and it is replaced by a backup-first, three-guard form. Full result: `snapshots/20260905-1910__Faz3.11__F31110-readback-result.txt`. **Phase 3 is closed.**
